Cover art by Jack Kirby and <a href="/wiki/Sol_Brodsky" title="Sol Brodsky">Sol Brodsky</a>.</div></div></div></div></div>\n<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Origins">Origins</span></h3>\n<p><b>Anthony Edward Stark</b> is the son of wealthy industrialist and head of <a href="/wiki/Stark_Industries" title="Stark Industries">Stark Industries</a>, <a href="/wiki/Howard_Stark" title="Howard Stark">Howard Stark</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Maria_Stark" title="Maria Stark">Maria Stark</a>. A boy genius, he enters <a href="/wiki/Massachusetts_Institute_of_Technology" title="Massachusetts Institute of Technology">MIT</a> at the age of 15 to study <a href="/wiki/Engineering" title="Engineering">engineering</a> and later receives master\'s degrees in engineering and physics. After his parents are killed in a car accident, he inherits his father\'s company.\n</p><p>Stark is injured by a booby trap and captured by enemy forces led by <a href="/wiki/Wong-Chu" title="Wong-Chu">Wong-Chu</a>. Wong-Chu orders Stark to build weapons, but Stark\'s injuries are dire and shrapnel is moving towards his heart. His fellow prisoner, <a href="/wiki/Ho_Yinsen" title="Ho Yinsen">Ho Yinsen</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Nobel_Prize" title="Nobel Prize">Nobel Prize</a>-winning <a href="/wiki/Physicist" title="Physicist">physicist</a> whose work Stark had greatly admired during college, constructs a <a href="/wiki/Magnetism" title="Magnetism">magnetic</a> chest plate to keep the shrapnel from reaching Stark\'s heart. In secret, Stark and Yinsen use the workshop to design and construct a <a href="/wiki/Iron_Man%27s_armor" title="Iron Man&#39;s armor">suit</a> of <a href="/wiki/Powered_exoskeleton" title="Powered exoskeleton">powered armor</a>, which Stark uses to escape. During the escape attempt, Yinsen sacrifices his life to save Stark\'s by distracting the enemy as Stark recharges. Stark takes revenge on his kidnappers and rejoins the American forces, on his way meeting a wounded American <a href="/wiki/United_States_Marine_Corps" title="United States Marine Corps">Marine</a> fighter pilot, <a href="/wiki/War_Machine" title="War Machine">James "Rhodey" Rhodes</a>.\n</p><p>Back home, Stark discovers that the shrapnel fragment lodged in his chest cannot be removed without killing him, and he is forced to wear the armor\'s chestplate beneath his clothes to act as a regulator for his heart. He must recharge the chestplate every day or else risk the shrapnel killing him. The <a href="/wiki/Cover-up" title="Cover-up">cover story</a> that Stark tells the news media and general public is that Iron Man is his robotic personal <a href="/wiki/Bodyguard" title="Bodyguard">bodyguard</a>, and corporate mascot. To that end, Iron Man fights threats to his company (e.g., <a href="/wiki/Communism" title="Communism">Communist</a> opponents <a href="/wiki/Black_Widow_(Natasha_Romanova)" title="Black Widow (Natasha Romanova)">Black Widow</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Crimson_Dynamo" title="Crimson Dynamo">Crimson Dynamo</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Titanium_Man" title="Titanium Man">Titanium Man</a>), as well as independent villains like the <a href="/wiki/Mandarin_(comics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mandarin (comics)">Mandarin</a> (who becomes his greatest enemy). No one suspects Stark of being Iron Man, as he cultivates a strong public image of being a rich playboy and industrialist. Two notable members of the series\' supporting cast, at this point, are his personal chauffeur <a href="/wiki/Happy_Hogan_(comics)" title="Happy Hogan (comics)">Harold "Happy" Hogan</a>, and secretary <a href="/wiki/Pepper_Potts" title="Pepper Potts">Virginia "Pepper" Potts</a>\xe2\x80\x94to both of whom he eventually reveals his dual identity. Meanwhile, James Rhodes finds his own niche as Stark\'s personal pilot, ultimately revealing himself to be a man of extraordinary skill and daring in his own right.\n</p><p>The series took an anti-Communist stance in its early years, which was softened as public (and therefore, presumably, reader) opposition rose to the <a href="/wiki/Vietnam_War" title="Vietnam War">Vietnam War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-origins_45_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-origins_45-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> This change evolved in a series of storylines featuring Stark reconsidering his political opinions, and the morality of manufacturing weapons for the U.S. military. Stark shows himself to be occasionally arrogant, and willing to act unethically in order to \'let the ends justify the means\'.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> This leads to personal conflicts with the people around him, both in his civilian and superhero identities. Stark uses his vast personal fortune not only to outfit his own armor, but also to develop weapons for <a href="/wiki/S.H.I.E.L.D." title="S.H.I.E.L.D.">S.H.I.E.L.D.</a>; other technologies (e.g., <a href="/wiki/Quinjet" class="mw-redirect" title="Quinjet">Quinjets</a> used by <a href="/wiki/Avengers_(comics)" title="Avengers (comics)">the Avengers</a>); and the image inducers used by the <a href="/wiki/X-Men" title="X-Men">X-Men</a>. Eventually, Stark\'s heart condition is resolved with an artificial <a href="/wiki/Heart_transplantation" title="Heart transplantation">heart transplant</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="1970s_and_early_1980s">1970s and early 1980s</span></h3>\n<p>Stark expands on his armor designs and begins to build his arsenal of specialized armors for particular situations such as for <a href="/wiki/Human_spaceflight" title="Human spaceflight">space travel</a><sup id="cite_ref-IronMan142_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan142-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Stealth_technology" title="Stealth technology">stealth</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DeFalco202_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DeFalco202-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-IronMan152_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan152-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Stark also develops a serious dependency on <a href="/wiki/Alcoholism" title="Alcoholism">alcohol</a> in the "<a href="/wiki/Demon_in_a_Bottle" title="Demon in a Bottle">Demon in a Bottle</a>" storyline.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> The first time it becomes a problem is when Stark discovers that the national security agency <a href="/wiki/S.H.I.E.L.D." title="S.H.I.E.L.D.">S.H.I.E.L.D.</a> has been buying a controlling interest in his company in order to ensure Stark\'s continued weapons development for them. At the same time, it is revealed that several minor supervillains armed with advanced weapons who had bedeviled Stark throughout his superhero career are in fact in the employ of Stark\'s business rival, <a href="/wiki/Justin_Hammer" title="Justin Hammer">Justin Hammer</a>, who begins to plague Stark more directly.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-IronMan124_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan124-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> At one point in Hammer\'s manipulations, the Iron Man armor is taken over and used to murder a diplomat.<sup id="cite_ref-IronMan124_43-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan124-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> Although Iron Man is not immediately under suspicion, Stark is forced to hand the armor over to the authorities.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> Eventually Stark and Rhodes, who is now his personal pilot and confidant, track down and defeat those responsible, although Hammer would return to bedevil Stark again.<sup id="cite_ref-IronMan142_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan142-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> With the support of his then-girlfriend, <a href="/wiki/Bethany_Cabe" title="Bethany Cabe">Bethany Cabe</a>, his friends and his employees, Stark pulls through these crises and overcomes his dependency on alcohol.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Even as he recovers from this harrowing personal trial, Stark\'s life is further complicated when he has a confrontation with <a href="/wiki/Doctor_Doom" title="Doctor Doom">Doctor Doom</a> that is interrupted by an opportunistic enemy sending them back in time to the time of <a href="/wiki/King_Arthur_(Marvel_Comics)" title="King Arthur (Marvel Comics)">King Arthur</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-IronMan149_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan149-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> Once there, Iron Man thwarts Doom\'s attempt to solicit the aid of <a href="/wiki/Morgan_le_Fay_(Marvel_Comics)" title="Morgan le Fay (Marvel Comics)">Morgan Le Fay</a>, and the Latverian ruler swears deadly vengeance\xe2\x80\x94to be indulged sometime after the two return to their own time.<sup id="cite_ref-IronMan150_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan150-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> This incident was collected and published as <i><a href="/wiki/Doomquest" title="Doomquest">Doomquest</a>.</i>\n</p><p>Some time later, a ruthless rival, <a href="/wiki/Iron_Monger" title="Iron Monger">Obadiah Stane</a>, manipulates Stark emotionally into a serious relapse. As a result, Stark loses control of Stark International to Stane, becomes a homeless alcoholic vagrant and gives up his armored identity to Rhodes, who becomes the new Iron Man. Eventually, Stark recovers and joins a new startup, Circuits Maximus. Stark concentrates on new technological designs, including building a new set of armor as part of his recuperative therapy. Rhodes continues to act as Iron Man but steadily grows more aggressive and paranoid, due to the armor not having been calibrated properly for his use. Eventually Rhodes goes on a rampage, and Stark has to don a replica of his original armor to stop him. Fully recovered, Stark confronts Stane who has himself designed armor based on designs seized along with Stark International, dubbing himself the \'Iron Monger\'. Defeated in battle, Stane, rather than give Stark the satisfaction of taking him to trial, commits suicide.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Shortly thereafter, Stark regains his personal fortune, but decides against repurchasing Stark International until much later; he instead creates Stark Enterprises, headquartered in <a href="/wiki/Los_Angeles" title="Los Angeles">Los Angeles</a>.\n</p>\n<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Late_1980s_and_1990s">Late 1980s and 1990s</span></h3>\n<p>In an attempt to stop other people from misusing his designs, Stark goes about <a href="/wiki/Armor_Wars" title="Armor Wars">disabling other armored heroes and villains</a> who are using suits based on the Iron Man technology, the designs of which were stolen by his enemy <a href="/wiki/Spymaster_(comics)#Spymaster_I" title="Spymaster (comics)">Spymaster</a>. His quest to destroy the stolen technology\xe2\x80\x94originally called "Stark Wars" but is more commonly known as the "<a href="/wiki/Armor_Wars" title="Armor Wars">Armor Wars</a>"\xe2\x80\x94severely hurts his reputation as Iron Man. After attacking and disabling a series of minor villains such as <a href="/wiki/Stilt-Man" title="Stilt-Man">Stilt-Man</a>, he attacks and defeats the government operative known as <a href="/wiki/Stingray_(comics)" title="Stingray (comics)">Stingray</a>. The situation worsens when Stark realizes that Stingray\'s armor does not incorporate any of his designs. He publicly "fires" Iron Man while covertly pursuing his agenda. He uses the cover story of wanting to help disable the rogue Iron Man to infiltrate and disable the armor of the S.H.I.E.L.D. operatives known as the <a href="/wiki/Mandroid" title="Mandroid">Mandroids</a>, as well as the armor of the <a href="/wiki/Guardsman_(comics)" title="Guardsman (comics)">Guardsmen</a>. In the process, Iron Man and Jim Rhodes allow some of the villains in the <a href="/wiki/Vault_(comics)" title="Vault (comics)">Vault</a> to escape. This leads the <a href="/wiki/Federal_government_of_the_United_States" title="Federal government of the United States">United States government</a> to declare Iron Man a danger and an outlaw, and severely sours Stark\'s relationship with Steve Rogers (<a href="/wiki/Captain_America" title="Captain America">Captain America</a>, who was in his "Captain" persona at the time). Iron Man travels to Russia where he inadvertently causes the death of the Soviet <a href="/wiki/Titanium_Man#Titanium_Man_II_-_The_Gremlin" title="Titanium Man">Titanium Man</a> during a fight. Returning to the U.S., he faces an enemy commissioned by the government named <a href="/wiki/Firepower_(comics)" title="Firepower (comics)">Firepower</a>. Unable to defeat him head on, Stark fakes Iron Man\'s demise,<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> intending to retire the suit permanently. When Firepower goes rogue, Stark creates a new suit, claiming a new person is in the armor.\n</p><p>Soon after, Stark is nearly killed by Kathy Dare, a mentally unbalanced former lover. She shoots him dead center in his torso which injures his spine, paralyzing him.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Stark undergoes special surgery to have a nerve chip implanted into his spine to regain his mobility.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Unbeknownst to the industrialist, the nerve chip is a clandestine means by which to gain control over his body. Rival businessmen the Marrs Twins and their cohort <a href="/wiki/Kearson_DeWitt" title="Kearson DeWitt">Kearson DeWitt</a> are behind the machinations in what came to be known as "Armor Wars II."<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> After several successful tests by DeWitt at manipulating Stark, Tony finds that using his Encephalo Armor can counteract DeWitt\'s controls.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> In response, DeWitt suddenly releases his control resulting in excruciating agony throughout Stark\'s body.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> The constant "battle" for control of Stark\'s nervous system and subsequent abdication on DeWitt\'s end lead to massive nerve damage throughout Tony\'s body. Stark\'s nervous system continues its slide towards failure, and he constructs a "skin" made up of artificial nerve circuitry to assist it.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Stark begins to pilot a remote-controlled Iron Man armor, but when faced with the Masters of Silence, the <a href="/wiki/Telepresence" title="Telepresence">telepresence</a> suit proves inadequate.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Stark designs a more heavily armed version of the suit to wear, the "Variable Threat Response Battle Suit", which becomes known as the <a href="/wiki/War_Machine" title="War Machine">War Machine armor</a>. Ultimately, the damage to his nervous system becomes too extensive. Faking his death, Stark places himself in <a href="/wiki/Suspended_animation" title="Suspended animation">suspended animation</a> to heal as Rhodes takes over both the running of Stark Enterprises and the mantle of Iron Man, although he uses the War Machine armor.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Stark eventually makes a full recovery by using a chip to create an entirely new (artificial) nervous system, and resumes as Iron Man in a new <a href="/wiki/Iron_Man%27s_armor#NTU-150_Telepresence_Armor" title="Iron Man&#39;s armor">Telepresence Armor</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> When Rhodes learns that Stark has manipulated his friends by faking his own death, he becomes enraged and the two friends part ways. Rhodes continues on as War Machine in a solo career.\n</p><p>The Avengers story arc "The Crossing" reveals Iron Man as a traitor among the team\'s ranks, due to years of manipulation by the time-traveling dictator <a href="/wiki/Kang_the_Conqueror" title="Kang the Conqueror">Kang the Conqueror</a>. Stark, as a sleeper agent in Kang\'s thrall, kills Marilla, the nanny of <a href="/wiki/Crystal_(comics)" title="Crystal (comics)">Crystal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Quicksilver_(comics)" title="Quicksilver (comics)">Quicksilver\'s</a> daughter Luna, as well as Rita DeMara, the female <a href="/wiki/Yellowjacket_(Rita_DeMara)" title="Yellowjacket (Rita DeMara)">Yellowjacket</a>, then Amanda Chaney, an ally of the Avengers. The "<a href="/wiki/Avengers_Forever" title="Avengers Forever">Avengers Forever</a>" limited series <a href="/wiki/Retroactive_continuity" title="Retroactive continuity">retcons</a> these events as the work of a disguised <a href="/wiki/Immortus" title="Immortus">Immortus</a>, not Kang, and that the mental control had gone back only a few months.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>Needing help to defeat both Stark and Kang, the team travels back in time to recruit a teenaged Anthony Stark from an alternate timeline to assist them. The young Stark steals an Iron Man suit in order to aid the Avengers against his older self. The sight of his younger self shocks the older Stark enough for him to regain momentary control of his actions, and he sacrifices his life to stop Kang.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> The young Stark later builds his own suit to become the new Iron Man and remains in the present day.\n</p><p>During the battle with the creature called <a href="/wiki/Onslaught_(comics)" title="Onslaught (comics)">Onslaught</a>, the teenage Stark dies, along with many other superheroes. <a href="/wiki/Franklin_Richards_(Fantastic_Four)" class="mw-redirect" title="Franklin Richards (Fantastic Four)">Franklin Richards</a> preserves these "dead" heroes in the "<a href="/wiki/Heroes_Reborn_(comics)" title="Heroes Reborn (comics)">Heroes Reborn</a>" pocket universe, in which Stark is once again an adult hero; Franklin recreates the heroes in the pocket universe in the forms he is most familiar with rather than what they are at the present. The reborn adult Stark, upon returning to the normal Marvel Universe, merges with the original Stark, who had died during "The Crossing", but was resurrected by Franklin Richards. This new Anthony Stark possesses the memories of both the original and teenage Anthony Stark, and thus considers himself to be essentially both of them. With the aid of the law firm <a href="/wiki/Daredevil_(Marvel_Comics_character)" title="Daredevil (Marvel Comics character)">Nelson &amp; Murdock</a>, he regains his fortune and, with Stark Enterprises having been sold to the Fujikawa Corporation following Stark\'s death, sets up a new company, Stark Solutions. He returns from the pocket universe with a restored and healthy heart. After the Avengers reform, Stark demands a hearing be convened to look into his actions just prior to the Onslaught incident. Cleared of wrongdoing, he rejoins the Avengers.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="2000s">2000s</span></h3>\n<p>At one point, Stark\'s armor becomes sentient despite fail-safes to prevent its increasingly sophisticated computer systems from doing so.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Initially, Stark welcomes this "living" armor for its improved tactical abilities. The armor begins to grow more aggressive, killing indiscriminately and eventually desiring to replace Stark altogether. In the final confrontation on a desert island, Stark suffers another heart attack. The armor sacrifices its own existence to save its creator\'s life, giving up essential components to give Stark a new, artificial heart. This new heart solves Stark\'s health problems, but it does not have an internal power supply, so Stark becomes once again dependent on periodic recharging. The sentient armor incident so disturbs Stark that he temporarily returns to using an unsophisticated early model version of his armor to avoid a repeat incident.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> He dabbles with using liquid metal circuitry known as S.K.I.N. that forms into a protective shell around his body, but eventually returns to more conventional hard metal armors.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>During this time, Stark engages in a romance with <a href="/wiki/Rumiko_Fujikawa" title="Rumiko Fujikawa">Rumiko Fujikawa</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> a wealthy heiress and daughter of the man who had taken over his company during the "Heroes Reborn" period. Her relationship with Stark endures many highs and lows, including infidelity with Stark\'s rival, Tiberius Stone, in part because the fun-loving Rumiko believes that Stark is too serious and dull. Their relationship ends with Rumiko\'s death at the hands of an Iron Man impostor in <i>Iron Man</i> (vol. 3) #87.\n</p><p>In <i>Iron Man</i> (vol. 3) #55 (July 2002), Stark publicly reveals his dual identity as Iron Man, not realizing that by doing so, he has invalidated the agreements protecting his armor from government duplication, since those contracts state that the Iron Man armor would be used by an <i>employee</i> of Tony Stark, not by Stark himself. When he discovers that the <a href="/wiki/Military_of_the_United_States" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of the United States">United States military</a> is again using his technology, and its defective nature nearly causes a disaster in Washington, D.C. which Iron Man barely manages to avert, Stark accepts a <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_United_States" title="President of the United States">Presidential</a> appointment as <a href="/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_Defense" title="United States Secretary of Defense">Secretary of Defense</a>. In this way, he hopes to monitor and direct how his designs are used.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>In the "<a href="/wiki/Avengers_Disassembled" title="Avengers Disassembled">Avengers Disassembled</a>" storyline, Stark is forced to resign after launching into a tirade against the <a href="/wiki/Latveria" title="Latveria">Latverian</a> ambassador at the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, being manipulated by the mentally imbalanced <a href="/wiki/Scarlet_Witch" title="Scarlet Witch">Scarlet Witch</a>, who destroys Avengers Mansion and kills several members. Stark publicly stands down as Iron Man, but continues using the costume. He joins the Avengers in stopping the breakout in progress from the Raft and even saves Captain America from falling.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> Tony changes the Avengers base to <a href="/wiki/Stark_Tower" title="Stark Tower">Stark Tower</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Ghost_(Marvel_Comics)" title="Ghost (Marvel Comics)">Ghost</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Living_Laser" title="Living Laser">Living Laser</a> and <a href="/wiki/Spymaster_(comics)" title="Spymaster (comics)">Spymaster</a> reappear and shift Iron Man from standard superhero stories to dealing with <a href="/wiki/Politics" title="Politics">politics</a> and <a href="/wiki/Industrialism" class="mw-redirect" title="Industrialism">industrialism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/Illuminati_(comics)" title="Illuminati (comics)">New Avengers: Illuminati</a></i> #1 (June 2006) reveals that years before, Stark had participated with a secret group that included the <a href="/wiki/Black_Panther_(comics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Black Panther (comics)">Black Panther</a>, <a href="/wiki/Professor_X" title="Professor X">Professor X</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mister_Fantastic" title="Mister Fantastic">Mister Fantastic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Black_Bolt" title="Black Bolt">Black Bolt</a>, <a href="/wiki/Doctor_Strange" title="Doctor Strange">Doctor Strange</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Namor" title="Namor">Namor</a>. The goal of the group (dubbed the <a href="/wiki/Illuminati_(comics)" title="Illuminati (comics)">Illuminati</a> by Marvel) was to strategize overarching menaces, in which the Black Panther rejects a membership offer. Stark\'s goal is to create a governing body for all superheroes in the world, but the beliefs of its members instead force them all to share vital information.\n</p>\n<h4><span id=".22Civil_War.22"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="&quot;Civil_War&quot;">"Civil War"</span></h4>\n<p>In the "<a href="/wiki/Civil_War_(comics)" title="Civil War (comics)">Civil War</a>" storyline, after the actions of inexperienced superheroes <a href="/wiki/The_New_Warriors" class="mw-redirect" title="The New Warriors">the New Warriors</a> result in the destruction of several city blocks in Stamford, Connecticut, there is an outcry across America against superhumans. Learning of the Government\'s proposed plans, Tony Stark suggests a new plan to instigate a <a href="/wiki/Superhuman_Registration_Act" class="mw-redirect" title="Superhuman Registration Act">Superhuman Registration Act</a>. The Act would force every superpowered individual in the U.S. to register their identity with the government and act as licensed agents. The Act would force inexperienced superhumans to receive training in how to use and control their abilities, something in which Tony strongly believes. Since his struggle with alcoholism, Stark has carried a tremendous burden of guilt after nearly killing an innocent bystander while piloting the armor drunk. While Reed Richards and <a href="/wiki/Hank_Pym" title="Hank Pym">Dr. Henry "Hank" Pym</a> both agree with Stark\'s proposal, not everyone does. After <a href="/wiki/Captain_America" title="Captain America">Captain America</a> is ordered to bring in anyone who refuses to register, he and other anti-registration superheroes go rogue, coming into conflict with the pro-registration heroes, led by Iron Man. The war ends when Captain America surrenders to prevent further collateral damage and civilian casualties, although he had defeated Stark by defusing his armor. Stark is appointed the new director of S.H.I.E.L.D.,<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> and organizes a new government-sanctioned group of <a href="/wiki/The_Mighty_Avengers" title="The Mighty Avengers">Avengers</a>. Shortly afterwards, Captain America is assassinated while in custody.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> This leaves Stark with a great amount of guilt and misgivings about the cost of his victory.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h4><span id=".22Secret_Invasion.22"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="&quot;Secret_Invasion&quot;">"Secret Invasion"</span></h4>\n<p>To tie into the 2008 <i><a href="/wiki/Iron_Man_(2008_film)" title="Iron Man (2008 film)">Iron Man</a></i> feature film, Marvel launched a new <i>Iron Man</i> ongoing series, <i>The Invincible Iron Man</i>, with writer <a href="/wiki/Matt_Fraction" title="Matt Fraction">Matt Fraction</a> and artist <a href="/wiki/Salvador_Larroca" title="Salvador Larroca">Salvador Larroca</a>. The series inaugural six-part storyline was "The Five Nightmares", which saw Stark targeted by <a href="/wiki/Ezekiel_Stane" class="mw-redirect" title="Ezekiel Stane">Ezekiel Stane</a>, the son of Stark\'s former nemesis, Obadiah Stane.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>In the "<a href="/wiki/Secret_Invasion" title="Secret Invasion">Secret Invasion</a>" storyline, after Tony Stark survives an attempt by <a href="/wiki/Ultron" title="Ultron">Ultron</a> to take over his body, he is confronted in the hospital by <a href="/wiki/Spider-Woman_(Jessica_Drew)" title="Spider-Woman (Jessica Drew)">Spider-Woman</a>, holding the corpse of a <a href="/wiki/Skrull" title="Skrull">Skrull</a> posing as <a href="/wiki/Elektra_(comics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Elektra (comics)">Elektra</a>. Realizing this is the start of an invasion by the Skrulls, Tony reveals the corpse to the Illuminati and declares that they are at war. After <a href="/wiki/Black_Bolt" title="Black Bolt">Black Bolt</a> reveals himself as a Skrull and is killed by <a href="/wiki/Namor" title="Namor">Namor</a>, a squadron of Skrulls attack, forcing Tony to evacuate the other Illuminati members and destroy the area, killing all the Skrulls. Realizing that they are incapable of trusting each other, the members all separate to form individual plans for the oncoming invasion.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>Stark is discredited and publicly vilified after his inability to anticipate or prevent the <a href="/wiki/Secret_Invasion" title="Secret Invasion">secret infiltration and invasion</a> of Earth by the Skrulls, and by the Skrull disabling of his StarkTech technology, which had a virtual monopoly on worldwide defense.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> After the invasion, the U.S. government removes him as head of <a href="/wiki/S.H.I.E.L.D." title="S.H.I.E.L.D.">S.H.I.E.L.D.</a> and disbands the <a href="/wiki/Avengers_(comics)" title="Avengers (comics)">Avengers</a>, handing control of the <a href="/wiki/Fifty_State_Initiative" class="mw-redirect" title="Fifty State Initiative">Initiative</a> over to <a href="/wiki/Green_Goblin" title="Green Goblin">Norman Osborn</a>.\n</p>\n<h4><span id=".22Dark_Reign.22"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="&quot;Dark_Reign&quot;">"Dark Reign"</span></h4>\n<div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Dark_Reign_(comics)" title="Dark Reign (comics)">Dark Reign (comics)</a></div>\n<div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/The_Invincible_Iron_Man" title="The Invincible Iron Man">The Invincible Iron Man</a></div>\n<p>With his Extremis powers failing, Stark uploads a virus to destroy all records of the Registration Act, thus preventing Osborn from learning the identities of his fellow heroes and anything that Osborn could use, including his repulsor generators. The only copy of the database is in Stark\'s brain, which he tries to delete while on the run from Osborn.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Stark goes so far as to inflict <a href="/wiki/Brain_damage" title="Brain damage">brain damage</a> on himself in order to ensure that the relevant information is wiped. When Osborn catches up to the debilitated Stark and beats him savagely, <a href="/wiki/Pepper_Potts" title="Pepper Potts">Pepper Potts</a> broadcasts the beatings worldwide, costing Osborn credibility and giving Stark public sympathy. Stark goes into a vegetative state, having previously granted <a href="/wiki/Thor_(Marvel_Comics)" title="Thor (Marvel Comics)">Donald Blake</a> (alter ego of the superhero Thor) power of attorney.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> A holographic message stored in Pepper\'s armor reveals that Stark had developed a means of \'rebooting\' his mind from his current state prior to his destruction of the database, with Blake and Bucky resolving to use it to restore him to normal. Meanwhile, Stark is trapped in his subconscious, where figments of his own mind prevent him from returning to the waking world. When the procedure fails to work, Bucky calls in Doctor Strange, who succeeds in restoring Stark back to consciousness. The backup Stark created was made prior to the Civil War, and as such he does not remember anything that took place during the event, although he still concludes after reviewing his past actions that he would not have done anything differently. His brain damage means he is now dependent on an arc reactor to sustain his body\'s autonomous functions.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="2010s">2010s</span></h3>\n<h4><span id=".22Siege.22"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="&quot;Siege&quot;">"Siege"</span></h4>\n<div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Siege_(comics)" title="Siege (comics)">Siege (comics)</a></div>\n<p>In the "<a href="/wiki/Siege_(comics)" title="Siege (comics)">Siege</a>" storyline, Tony Stark is seen under the care of <a href="/wiki/Thor_(Marvel_Comics)" title="Thor (Marvel Comics)">Dr. Donald Blake</a> and <a href="/wiki/Maria_Hill" title="Maria Hill">Maria Hill</a> when Asgard is attacked.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Thor_(Marvel_Comics)" title="Thor (Marvel Comics)">Thor</a> is ambushed by <a href="/wiki/Green_Goblin" title="Green Goblin">Osborn</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Sentry_(Robert_Reynolds)" title="Sentry (Robert Reynolds)">Sentry</a>, but rescued by Hill. Osborn declares <a href="/wiki/Martial_law" title="Martial law">martial law</a> and unleashes <a href="/wiki/Daken" title="Daken">Daken</a> and the Sentry on Broxton to root out Thor and Hill. Hill returns to Stark\'s hiding place to move him to a safer location and are joined by <a href="/wiki/Speed_(comics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Speed (comics)">Speed</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Young_Avengers" title="Young Avengers">Young Avengers</a>, who has a set of Iron Man\'s MK III armor that <a href="/wiki/Edwin_Jarvis" title="Edwin Jarvis">Edwin Jarvis</a> had given Captain America.  While Osborn is battling the <a href="/wiki/The_New_Avengers_(comics)" title="The New Avengers (comics)">New Avengers</a>, Stark appears and disables Osborn\'s Iron Patriot armor. Osborn orders the Sentry to annihilate Asgard, rather than allow the Avengers to have it. After Asgard falls, Stark stands alongside his fellow heroes, as Osborn exclaims they are all doomed and he \'was saving them from him\' pointing up towards a <a href="/wiki/Sentry_(Robert_Reynolds)#Void" title="Sentry (Robert Reynolds)">Void</a>-possessed Sentry.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> As the Void tears apart the teams, Loki gives them the power to fight back through the <a href="/wiki/Norn_Stones" title="Norn Stones">Norn Stones</a>. The Void kills Loki, enraging Thor. Tony tells Thor to get the Void away from Asgard, which allows Tony to drop a commandeered H.A.M.M.E.R. Helicarrier on the Void.  Thor is forced to killed Sentry when the Void resurfaces. Sometime later, the Super-Human Registration Act is repealed and Tony is given back his company and armor. As a symbol for their heroics and their new unity, Thor places an Asgardian tower on Stark Tower where the Watchtower once stood.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h4><span id=".22Heroic_Age.22"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="&quot;Heroic_Age&quot;">"Heroic Age"</span></h4>\n<div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Heroic_Age_(comics)" title="Heroic Age (comics)">Heroic Age (comics)</a></div>\n<p>In the 2010\xe2\x80\x932011 "Stark: Resilient" storyline, Tony builds the Bleeding Edge armor with the help of Mister Fantastic. This new armor fully uses the repulsor tech battery embedded in his chest to power Tony\'s entire body and mind, thus allowing him access to Extremis once more. Furthermore, the battery operates as his "heart" and is the only thing keeping him alive.<sup id="cite_ref-Fraction,_Matt_2010_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fraction,_Matt_2010-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Tony announces he will form a new company, Stark Resilient. He states that he will no longer develop weapons, but will use his repulsor technology to give free energy to the world. Justine and Sasha Hammer create their own armored hero, Detroit Steel, to take Stark\'s place as the Army\'s leading weapons-builder. Stark\'s plan consists of building two repulsor-powered cars. The Hammers try to foil his efforts. The first car is destroyed by sabotage, while Detroit Steel attacks Stark Resilient\'s facilities while Tony tests the second car. Through a legal maneuver, Tony is able to get the Hammers to stop their attacks and releases a successful commercial about his new car.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h4><span id=".22Fear_Itself.22"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="&quot;Fear_Itself&quot;">"Fear Itself"</span></h4>\n<p>In the 2011 "<a href="/wiki/Fear_Itself_(comics)" title="Fear Itself (comics)">Fear Itself</a>" storyline, Earth is attacked by the <a href="/wiki/Serpent_(comics)" title="Serpent (comics)">Serpent</a>, the God of Fear and the long-forgotten brother of Odin.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> In Paris, Iron Man fights <a href="/wiki/Grey_Gargoyle" title="Grey Gargoyle">Grey Gargoyle</a>, who has become Mokk, Breaker of Faith, one of the Serpent\'s Worthy. Mokk leaves Iron Man unconscious and transforms Detroit Steel and the citizens of Paris into stone.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> To defeat the Serpent\'s army, Tony drinks a bottle of wine (thus \'sacrificing\' his sobriety) to gain an audience with Odin, who allows Tony to enter the realm of Svartalfheim. Tony and the dwarves of Svartalfheim build enchanted weapons.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> Tony upgrades his armor with uru-infused enchantments and delivers the finished weapons to the Avengers, who use them for the final battle against the Serpent\'s forces. Iron Man watches as Thor kills the Serpent, but dies in the process. After the battle is over, Tony melts down the weapons he created and repairs Captain America\'s shield, which had been broken by Serpent, and gives it back to Captain America.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> During a subsequent argument with Odin about the gods\' lack of involvement in the recent crisis, Odin gives Tony a brief opportunity to see the vastness of the universe the way he sees it.  As thanks for Tony\'s role in the recent crisis, Odin restores all the people that the Grey Gargoyle killed during his rampage.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h4><span id="Return_of_the_Mandarin_and_Marvel_NOW.21"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Return_of_the_Mandarin_and_Marvel_NOW!">Return of the Mandarin and Marvel NOW!</span></h4>\n<p>In the storylines "Demon" and "The Long Way Down", Stark is subpoenaed by the U.S. government after evidence surfaces of him using the Iron Man armor while under the influence. Mandarin and Zeke Stane upgrade some of Iron Man\'s old enemies and send them to commit acts of terrorism across the world, intending to discredit Iron Man. General Bruce Babbage forces Stark to wear a tech governor, a device that allows Babbage to deactivate Stark\'s armor whenever he wants. To fight back, Tony undergoes a surgical procedure that expels the Bleeding Edge technology out of his body and replaces his repulsor node with a new model, forcing Babbage to remove the tech governor off his chest. He announces his retirement as Iron Man, faking Rhodes\' death and giving him a new armor so that he becomes the new Iron Man.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> This leads into the next storyline, "The Future", in which the Mandarin takes control of Stark\'s mind and uses him to create new armored bodies for the alien spirits inhabiting his rings, but Stark allies himself with some of his old enemies, who have also been imprisoned by Mandarin, and manages to defeat him. The final issue of this storyline concluded Matt Fraction\'s series.<sup id="cite_ref-IronMan523_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan523-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>In the ongoing series that premiered in 2012 as part of the <a href="/wiki/Marvel_NOW!" title="Marvel NOW!">Marvel NOW!</a> relaunch, Tony Stark has hit a technological ceiling. After the death of Dr. Maya Hansen and the destruction of all of the Extremis Version 2 kits that were being sold to the black market, Tony decides that the Earth is not safe without him learning more from what\'s in the final frontier. He takes his new suit, enhanced with an artificial intelligence named P.E.P.P.E.R. and joins <a href="/wiki/Peter_Quill" class="mw-redirect" title="Peter Quill">Peter Quill</a> and The <a href="/wiki/Guardians_of_the_Galaxy_(2008_team)" title="Guardians of the Galaxy (2008 team)">Guardians of the Galaxy</a> after helping them thwart a Badoon attack on Earth.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Superior_Iron_Man">Superior Iron Man</span></h4>\n<p>Tony Stark\'s personality is inverted during the events of <i><a href="/wiki/AXIS_(comics)" title="AXIS (comics)">AXIS</a></i>, bringing out more dark aspects of himself like irresponsibility, egotism and alcoholism.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> Stark relocates to <a href="/wiki/San_Francisco" title="San Francisco">San Francisco</a> and builds a new, all-white armor.  He supplies the citizens of San Francisco with the Extremis 3.0 app, a version of the techno-virus that offers beauty, health or even immortality, free.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> When every person in the city viewed Iron Man as a messiah for making their dreams come true, he ended the free trial mode and started charging a daily fee of $99.99, causing many to resort to crime to pay for the upgrade.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Daredevil_(Marvel_Comics_character)" title="Daredevil (Marvel Comics character)">Daredevil</a> confronts Stark at his new <a href="/wiki/Alcatraz_Island" title="Alcatraz Island">Alcatraz Island</a> penthouse, but is easily brushed off.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Iron Man uses Extremis 3.0 to temporarily restore Daredevil\'s sight, just to prove his point.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> Daredevil deduces that Stark had added Extremis to the water supply and the phones only transmit an activation signal, but Stark subjects Murdock to minor brain damage to prevent him from sharing this revelation with others.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>After discovering that new villain Teen Abomination is the son of Happy Hogan, Stark decides to help him,<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> but this minor act of redemption is too late for Pepper Potts, who attacks Stark with the aid of an A.I. based on Stark\'s mind.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> This culminates in a confrontation between the two Starks, as Stark calls on the unwitting aid of all \'infected\' with the Extremis upgrade while the A.I. uses Stark\'s various old armors to attack him.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> Although Stark technically wins the battle as he destroys his other armors and deletes the A.I. backup, Pepper states that she plans to reveal the truth about his goals with Extremis, bluntly informing him that if he continues his Extremis upgrade project, he will have to do it alone, accepting his fate of being regarded as a monster by all who know him.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Time_Runs_Out">Time Runs Out</span></h4>\n<p>During the "<a href="/w/index.php?title=Time_Runs_Out&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Time Runs Out (page does not exist)">Time Runs Out</a>" storyline, an attempt at reclaiming Wakanda from the Cabal that Namor had created to destroy incursive Earths results in Tony being held captive in the Necropolis.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> After the Cabal are apparently killed, the Illuminati free Tony, who is forced to flee due to the Illuminati\'s unwillingness to let Stark be there with them when they meet Rogers and the Avengers. When the Shi\'ar and their allies arrive to destroy Earth, the Avengers and the Illuminati unsuccessfully try to retaliate. Iron Man uses Sol\'s Hammer to destroy the fleet.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> The incursions continue, and Rogers confronts Stark about what he knows. A fight ensues between them and Stark admits that he had lied and had known about the incursions all along. During the final incursion, Earth-1610\'s S.H.I.E.L.D. launches a full-scale attack on Earth-616, during which Stark and Rogers are crushed by a Helicarrier.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h4><span id="All-New.2C_All-Different_Marvel"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="All-New,_All-Different_Marvel">All-New, All-Different Marvel</span></h4>\n<p>After the events of the <i>Secret Wars</i> crossover, Stark returns to his normal self with no signs of his inverted personality. Eight months following the return of the universe as seen in the "<a href="/wiki/All-New,_All-Different_Marvel" title="All-New, All-Different Marvel">All-New, All-Different Marvel</a>" event, Tony works in his laboratory non-stop after his position as an innovator had been put in doubt. Because an M.I.T. student reverse-engineered some of his technology, Stark develops a new armor which can change shape according to the situation he would find himself. When Stark\'s new A.I. <a href="/wiki/F.R.I.D.A.Y." title="F.R.I.D.A.Y.">F.R.I.D.A.Y.</a> informs him that Madame Masque has broken into the ruins of Castle Doom, he travels to Latveria to invfestigate and runs into some revolutionaries who are then defeated by a man in a suit. To his amazement, Iron Man\'s armor computer identifies him as Doctor Doom with his face restored. Doctor Doom claims that he wanted to help Iron Man.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>After learning from Doctor Doom that Madame Masque has taken a decoy of the Wand of Watoomb, Tony Stark confronts Madame Masque. Upon learning that Madame Masque is not allied with Doctor Doom, Tony is attacked by her with a burst of energy that damages his armor.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> F.R.I.D.A.Y. manages to gain control of the suit and takes Tony to a safe location. Iron Man tracks Madame Masque to Marina del Rey. After finding a tape recorder with her messages, Tony is attacked by several black silhouettes with swords.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>Iron Man escapes the ninjas that are attacking him and manages to defeat most of them, but they kill themselves before he can interrogate any of them. Iron Man and Doctor Doom arrive at <a href="/wiki/Mary_Jane_Watson" title="Mary Jane Watson">Mary Jane Watson</a>\'s newest Chicago night club Jackpot when Madame Masque attacks it.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> As Mary Jane distracts Madame Masque by knocking off her mask, Iron Man and Doctor Doom discover that Madame Masque is possessed by a demon. Doctor Doom is able to perform an exorcism on her.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific volume in which the source appears. (April 2016)">volume&#160;&amp;&#160;issue&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>\n</p><p>Doctor Strange arrives and tells Iron Man he will take Madame Masque with him to fix her metaphysically and then hand her over at S.H.I.E.L.D. Iron Man also informs him of Doctor Doom\'s help who had left the scene some time ago. Three days later, Iron Man offers Mary Jane a job to make up for the damage to her nightclub.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> After speaking with War Machine, Tony Stark meets up at a diner with Amara Perera when they are unexpectedly joined by Doctor Doom who wanted to make sure that the demonic possession that affected Madame Masque has not affected Stark or Amara.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Stark shows Mary Jane the demonstration on the people that he will be working with. They are interrupted by F.R.I.D.A.Y. who tells Tony that War Machine is missing.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific volume in which the source appears. (April 2016)">volume&#160;&amp;&#160;issue&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Before heading to Tokyo, Tony receives from Mary Jane the emergency number for Peter Parker. In Tokyo, Iron Man is contacted by Spider-Man at War Machine\'s last known location as he is being observed by ninjas.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>During the <i><a href="/wiki/Civil_War_II" title="Civil War II">Civil War II</a></i> storyline, Iron Man protests the logic of using precognitive powers to stop future crimes after the recently emerged Inhuman <a href="/wiki/Ulysses_(comics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ulysses (comics)">Ulysses</a> predicted <a href="/wiki/Thanos" title="Thanos">Thanos</a>\'s attack on Project Pegasus. Three weeks later, Iron Man is summoned to the Triskelion after War Machine is killed in battle against Thanos. When Iron Man learns that War Machine and the Ultimates used Ulysses\' power to ambush Thanos, he vows to stop anyone from using that power again.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Iron Man infiltrates New Attilan and makes off with Ulysses. At Stark Tower, Iron Man vows to find out how Ulysses\' precognition works.  The Inhumans attack Stark Tower but are stopped by the Avengers, the <a href="/wiki/Ultimates_(2015_team)" title="Ultimates (2015 team)">Ultimates</a>, and S.H.I.E.L.D. During the confrontation, Ulysses has another vision which he projects to Iron Man and everyone present, showing a rampaging Hulk standing over the corpses of the defeated superheroes.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> The heroes confront Banner, who is killed by Hawkeye.  Barton claims that Banner was about to transform and Banner had previously asked Hawkeye to kill him if he should turn back into the Hulk. Tony is disgusted at this use of Ulysses\' power. When his analysis of Ulysses brain is completed,<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> Tony reveals that Ulysses does not actually see the <i>future</i>, but simply assembles large quantities of data to project likely outcomes. While Danvers continues to use the visions as a resource, Tony objects to the concept of profiling people. This results in a stand-off when Tony\'s side abducts a woman from custody after Ulysses\' visions identified her as a deep-cover <a href="/wiki/Hydra_(comics)" title="Hydra (comics)">HYDRA</a> agent, despite the lack of supporting evidence.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>Iron Man learns that his biological mother was actually <b>Amanda Armstrong</b>, who had given him up for adoption and that his biological father was a Hydra double agent named <b>Jude</b>. S.H.I.E.L.D. had Armstrong\'s baby adopted by Howard and Maria Stark.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h4><span id="Marvel_NOW.21_2016"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Marvel_NOW!_2016">Marvel NOW! 2016</span></h4>\n<p>In July 2016, it was announced that Tony Stark would hand off the mantle of Iron Man to a 15-year-old girl named <a href="/wiki/Ironheart_(comics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ironheart (comics)">Riri Williams</a>. Riri is an MIT student who built her own Iron Man suit out of scrap pieces and, as such, attracted Stark\'s attention. Early depictions of Williams\' suit depict it without the Arc Reactor, but leaves the power source for the suit unclear.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Another Iron Man-based series titled <i><a href="/wiki/Infamous_Iron_Man" title="Infamous Iron Man">Infamous Iron Man</a></i> debuted featuring Doctor Doom sporting his version of the Iron Man armor.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> This is revealed to be the result of serious injuries sustained by Stark during his final confrontation with Captain Marvel.  Danvers\' beating leaves Stark in a coma, but he is left alive due to unspecified experiments Stark has carried out on himself over the years.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Existing_as_an_A.I.">Existing as an A.I.</span></h4>\n<p>Following the revelation that Stark experimented on himself at the end of <i>Civil War II</i>, Beast concludes that the only option is to let the experiments do their job in healing Tony and recover on his own.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific volume in which the source appears. (June 2017)">volume&#160;&amp;&#160;issue&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In <i>Invincible Iron Man</i> #1, an employee of Stark sends Riri Williams an artificial intelligence housing a copy of Tony Stark\'s consciousness to help her control and mentor in her own version of the Iron Man armor. This A.I. is directly copied from Tony\'s brain, granting sentience, with Williams commenting on Stark existing as a "techno-ghost". As an A.I., Stark can walk around as a hard-light object and gains the ability to remote control his vast armory of Iron Man suits.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific volume in which the source appears. (March 2017)">volume&#160;&amp;&#160;issue&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In <i>The Mighty Captain Marvel</i> #3, the Tony Stark A.I. goes to Antarctica and visits Captain Marvel with the intent on settling their differences from the Second Civil War, she apologizes to him for her regrets, reconciles with him eventually and they become allies once more. Then in <a href="/wiki/Secret_Empire_(comics)" title="Secret Empire (comics)">Secret Empire</a>, the Tony Stark A.I. suits up as Iron Man once again and learns of Captain America\'s betrayal to Hydra and how he ended up like this. As Hydra tightens its grip across America, the Tony Stark A.I. leads a team known as the Underground to find the Cosmic Cubes to restore Rogers to his normal self. When things start to escalate, Tony and his crew go rogue and in search for answers for the Cube. As Iron Man and the Underground search for them they are intercepted by Captain America and his Hydra team. With both teams encountering each other, they are captured by the Ultron/Hank Pym hybrid, who forces both teams to sit at a dinner table. During "dinner", Ultron reveals information about the Hydra Avengers - such as Odinson working with Hydra to reclaim Mjolnir, Scarlet Witch being possessed by Chthon, and Vision being affected by an A.I. virus. Ultron argues that he is doing this because the Avengers have become less of a family over the years as so many of them jump to obey Captain America or Iron Man, despite past experience confirming that this is not always a good idea, but Tony counters that the only reason the team failed as a family was because of Hank\'s abuse towards Wasp. Enraged, Ultron is about to kill everyone, but Ant-Man is able to calm him down by arguing that Hank remains his own inspiration. Ultron allows the Underground to leave with the fragment, arguing that neither side should have an advantage over the other. Back in America, Hydra Supreme has put Namor in a position where he will be forced to sign a peace treaty that gives Rogers access to the Cosmic Cube fragment in Atlantis, but Hydra Supreme muses that he is unconcerned about who will acquire the fragments, as he has an inside man in the Underground. After the Mount was attacked by Thor and the resurrected Hulk led by Hydra, the Underground evacuated the civilians thanks to Hawkeye and the rest of the heroes. Captain America and Iron Man fight as the Mount collapses around them. The Tony Stark A.I. initiates the Mount\'s "Clean Slate Protocol", and blows up the Mount, killing Madame Hydra, then apologizes to Steve Rogers about their past differences, but the A.I survives and, in the aftermath, helps the heroes pull the pieces back together to take down Hydra. When Iron Man confronts Hydra Supreme, he and the other heroes are easily overpowered by him and watch the original Captain America defeat his Hydra self and into celebrating their victory.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Marvel_Legacy">Marvel Legacy</span></h4>\n<table class="box-Update plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Update" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div style="width:52px"><img alt="Ambox current red.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Ambox_current_red.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red.svg.png" decoding="async" width="42" height="34" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Ambox_current_red.svg/63px-Ambox_current_red.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Ambox_current_red.svg/84px-Ambox_current_red.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="360" data-file-height="290" /></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section needs to be <b>updated</b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information.</span>  <small class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">May 2019</span>)</i></small></div></td></tr></tbody></table>\n<p>Mary Jane Watson and other Stark employees find that Stark\'s body has completely vanished from its pod, despite tests taken mere hours ago showing no sign of improvement or brain activity.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific volume in which the source appears. (June 2018)">volume&#160;&amp;&#160;issue&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> It was discovered that Tony Stark\'s comatose body was taken to the Stark Industries Complex in Dover.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> Upon awakening when his biological systems rebooted, Tony Stark went into hiding until he fully recovered.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> Wanting to make sure that James Rhodes was in a similar state that he was in, Tony Stark exhumed his body from Arlington Cemetery and kick-started the biological systems in him. Afterwards, Iron Man and War Machine fought Hood to keep Stark Industries from falling into Hood\'s hands where he switched to a version of his Extremis armor. Then he proceeded to defeat Jude when he turned up alive.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h2><span id="Powers.2C_abilities.2C_and_equipment"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Powers,_abilities,_and_equipment">Powers, abilities, and equipment</span></h2>\n<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Armor">Armor</span></h3>\n<div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Iron_Man%27s_armor" title="Iron Man&#39;s armor">Iron Man\'s armor</a></div>\n<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:IronManV5No25.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/6/63/IronManV5No25.jpg/220px-IronManV5No25.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="94" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/6/63/IronManV5No25.jpg/330px-IronManV5No25.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/6/63/IronManV5No25.jpg/440px-IronManV5No25.jpg 2x" data-file-width="482" data-file-height="207" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:IronManV5No25.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Bleeding Edge Armor, like the Extremis Armor before it, is stored in Stark\'s bones, and can be assembled and controlled by his thoughts</div></div></div>\n<p>Iron Man possesses <a href="/wiki/Powered_exoskeleton" title="Powered exoskeleton">powered armor</a> that gives him superhuman strength and durability, flight, and an array of weapons. The armor is invented and worn by Stark (with occasional short-term exceptions). Other people who have assumed the Iron Man identity include Stark\'s long-time partner and best friend <a href="/wiki/War_Machine" title="War Machine">James Rhodes</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-DeFalco211_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DeFalco211-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> close associates <a href="/wiki/Happy_Hogan_(comics)" title="Happy Hogan (comics)">Harold "Happy" Hogan</a>; Eddie March;<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> (briefly) <a href="/wiki/Guardsman_(Michael_O%27Brien)" class="mw-redirect" title="Guardsman (Michael O&#39;Brien)">Michael O\'Brien</a> and Riri Williams.\n</p><p>The weapons systems of the suit have changed over the years, but Iron Man\'s standard offensive weapons have always been the repulsor rays that are fired from the palms of his <a href="/wiki/Gauntlet_(glove)" title="Gauntlet (glove)">gauntlets</a>. Other weapons built into various incarnations of the armor include: the uni-beam projector in its chest; pulse bolts (that pick up <a href="/wiki/Kinetic_energy" title="Kinetic energy">kinetic energy</a> along the way; so the farther they travel, the harder they hit); an <a href="/wiki/Electromagnetic_pulse" title="Electromagnetic pulse">electromagnetic pulse</a> generator; and a defensive energy shield that can be extended up to 360 degrees. Other capabilities include: generating ultra-<a href="/wiki/Freon" title="Freon">freon</a> (i.e., a freeze-beam); creating and manipulating <a href="/wiki/Magnetic_field" title="Magnetic field">magnetic fields</a>; emitting sonic blasts; and projecting 3-dimensional <a href="/wiki/Hologram" class="mw-redirect" title="Hologram">holograms</a> (to create decoys).\n</p><p>In addition to the general-purpose model he wears, Stark has developed several specialized <a href="/wiki/Space_suit" title="Space suit">suits</a> for <a href="/wiki/Spaceflight" title="Spaceflight">space travel</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-IronMan142_38-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan142-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Atmospheric_diving_suit" title="Atmospheric diving suit">deep-sea diving</a>, <a href="/wiki/Stealth_technology" title="Stealth technology">stealth</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-DeFalco202_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DeFalco202-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-IronMan152_40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan152-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> and other special purposes. Stark has modified suits, like the Hulkbuster heavy armor. The Hulkbuster armor is composed of add-ons to his so-called modular armor, designed to enhance its strength and durability enough to engage the <a href="/wiki/Hulk" title="Hulk">Hulk</a> in a fight. A later model, created with the help of <a href="/wiki/Odin_(Marvel_Comics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Odin (Marvel Comics)">Odin</a> and the Asgardian metal Uru, is similar to the <a href="/wiki/Destroyer_(Thor)" title="Destroyer (Thor)">Destroyer</a>. Stark develops an electronics pack during the Armor Wars that, when attached to armors that use Stark technologies, will burn out those components, rendering the suit useless. This pack is ineffective on later models. While it is typically associated with <a href="/wiki/War_Machine" title="War Machine">James Rhodes</a>, the War Machine armor began as one of Stark\'s specialty armors.\n</p><p>The most recent models of Stark\'s armor, beginning with the Extremis armor, are now stored in the hollow portions of Stark\'s bones, and the personal area networking implement used to control it is implanted into his forearm, and connected directly to his <a href="/wiki/Central_nervous_system" title="Central nervous system">central nervous system</a>.\n</p><p>The Extremis has since been removed,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific volume in which the source appears. (April 2014)">volume&#160;&amp;&#160;issue&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> and he now uses more conventional armors. Some armors still take a liquid form, but are not stored within his body. His Endo-Sym Armor incorporates a combination of the liquid smart-metal with the alien Venom symbiote, psionically controlled by Stark.\n</p><p>Post-<i>Secret Wars</i>, Stark uses a more streamlined suit of armor that uses <a href="/wiki/Nanotechnology" title="Nanotechnology">nanotechnology</a> to shape shift into other armors or weapons.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Powers">Powers</span></h3>\n<p>After being critically injured during a battle with the Extremis-enhanced Mallen, Stark injects his nervous system with modified techno-organic virus-like body restructuring machines (the Extremis process).<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> By rewriting his own biology, Stark is able to save his life, gain an enhanced <a href="/wiki/Healing_factor" class="mw-redirect" title="Healing factor">healing factor</a>, and partially merge with the Iron Man armor, superseding the need for bulky, AI-controlled armors in favor of lighter designs, technopathically controlled by his own brain. His enhanced technopathy extends to every piece of technology, limitless and effortlessly due to his ability to interface with communication satellites and wireless connections to increase his "range". Some components of the armor-sheath are now stored in Tony\'s body, able to be recalled, and extruded from his own skin, at will.\n</p><p>During the "<a href="/wiki/Secret_Invasion" title="Secret Invasion">Secret Invasion</a>" storyline the Extremis package is catastrophically shut down by a virus, forcing him again to rely on the previous iteration of his armor, and restoring his previous limitations. Furthermore, Osborn\'s takeover of most of the few remaining Starktech factories, with Ezekiel Stane systematically crippling the others, limits Tony to the use of lesser, older and weaker armors.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>After being forced to "wipe out" his brain to prevent Norman Osborn from gaining his information, Tony Stark is forced to have a new arc reactor, of <a href="/wiki/Iron_Fist_(comics)" title="Iron Fist (comics)">Rand</a> design installed in his chest. The process greatly improves his strength, stamina and intellect. The procedure left him with virtually no autonomic functions: as his brain was stripped of every biological function, Tony is forced to rely on a digital backup of his memories (leaving him with severe gaps and lapses in his long-term memory) and on software routine in the arc reactor for basic stimuli reaction, such as blinking and breathing.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> The <i>Bleeding Edge</i> package of armor and physical enhancement is now equal in power, if not a more advanced, version of the old Extremis tech.<sup id="cite_ref-Fraction,_Matt_2010_83-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fraction,_Matt_2010-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>\n</p>\n<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Skills">Skills</span></h3>\n<p>Tony Stark is an inventive genius whose expertise in the fields of <a href="/wiki/Mathematics" title="Mathematics">mathematics</a>, <a href="/wiki/Physics" title="Physics">physics</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chemistry" title="Chemistry">chemistry</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Computer_science" title="Computer science">computer science</a> rivals that of <a href="/wiki/Mister_Fantastic" title="Mister Fantastic">Reed Richards</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hank_Pym" title="Hank Pym">Hank Pym</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Hulk" title="Hulk">Bruce Banner</a>, and his expertise in <a href="/wiki/Electrical_engineering" title="Electrical engineering">electrical engineering</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mechanical_engineering" title="Mechanical engineering">mechanical engineering</a> surpasses even theirs. He is regarded as one of the most intelligent characters in the Marvel Universe. He graduated with advanced degrees in physics and engineering at the age of 17 from <a href="/wiki/Massachusetts_Institute_of_Technology" title="Massachusetts Institute of Technology">Massachusetts Institute of Technology</a> (MIT)<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> and further developed his knowledge ranging from <a href="/wiki/Artificial_intelligence" title="Artificial intelligence">artificial intelligence</a> to <a href="/wiki/Quantum_mechanics" title="Quantum mechanics">quantum mechanics</a> as time progressed. His expertise extends to his ingenuity in dealing with difficult situations, such as difficult foes and deathtraps, in which he is capable of using available tools, including his suit, in unorthodox but effective ways. He is well respected in the business world, able to command people\'s attention when he speaks on economic matters, having over the years built up several multimillion-dollar companies from virtually nothing. He is noted for the loyalty he commands from and returns to those who work for him, as well as for his business ethics. Thus he immediately fired an employee who made profitable, but illegal, sales to <a href="/wiki/Doctor_Doom" title="Doctor Doom">Doctor Doom</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-IronMan149_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IronMan149-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> He strives to be environmentally responsible in his businesses.\n</p><p>At a time when Stark was unable to use his armor for a period, he received some combat training from <a href="/wiki/Captain_America" title="Captain America">Captain America</a> and has become physically formidable on his own when the situation demands it.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> In addition, Stark possesses great business and political acumen. On multiple occasions he reacquired control of his companies after losing them amid corporate takeovers.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>\n</p><p>Due to his membership in the Illuminati, Iron Man was given the Space <a href="/wiki/Infinity_Gem" class="mw-redirect" title="Infinity Gem">Infinity Gem</a> to safeguard.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>  It allows the user to exist in any location (or all locations), move any object anywhere throughout the universe and warp or rearrange space.\n</p>\n<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Supporting_characters">Supporting characters</span></h2>\n<div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_Iron_Man_supporting_characters" title="List of Iron Man supporting characters">List of Iron Man supporting characters</a></div>\n<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_versions">Other versions</span></h2>\n<div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Alternative_versions_of_Iron_Man" title="Alternative versions of Iron Man">Alternative versions of Iron Man</a></div>\n<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_other_media">In other media</span></h2>\n<div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Iron_Man_in_other_media" title="Iron Man in other media">Iron Man in other media</a></div>\n<div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Tony_Stark_(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe)" title="Tony Stark (Marvel Cinematic Universe)">Tony Stark (Marvel Cinematic Universe)</a></div>\n<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Robert_Downey_Jr_as_Iron_Man.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/8/84/Robert_Downey_Jr_as_Iron_Man.jpg/170px-Robert_Downey_Jr_as_Iron_Man.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="284" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/8/84/Robert_Downey_Jr_as_Iron_Man.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="250" data-file-height="418" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Robert_Downey_Jr_as_Iron_Man.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Robert_Downey_Jr." title="Robert Downey Jr.">Robert Downey Jr.</a> as <a
